ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u2	Mr President, thank you for saying so. Dear Members, Members and others! The proposal to amend the Law on Prevention of Work and Black Employment has in fact only one key objective: to comply with a directive that has abolished the charging of consumer fees and commissions when there is a cash-free payment. How is this to be reflected or reflected in practice? It is the valuations paid for the acquisition in order to pay the corresponding contributions in this regard, and in this case one of the indents provided for in the law is deleted, which provides that this is the liability of the payee. So, in this case, the burden will pass to the state. It should be said that this is already being done in practice, on the basis that the Directive is being applied directly, which means that for some time now it has been considered that the UJP, which processes these payments, is also a burden, is saying that the payment of the contributions is already under the burden of the State on the basis of the possibility that the directive is directly applicable. In fact, this is simply a matter of harmonising the situation created by this Directive. Thank you very much.

The Committee also received the opinion of the Commission of the National Council for Social Protection, Labour, Health and Disabled Persons, which supports the proposed amendments. The Commission was aware that it was a formalisation of the changes already made, since the competent ministry of the Public Payments Office, as soon as the EU Directive was in force, gave an initiative to ensure that the fees to consumers for non-cash payments of payment cards are no longer charged in accordance with the provisions of that Directive. As amendments have not been adopted to the draft law on the home labour body, the Committee on Labour, the Family, Social Affairs and the Disability of the National Assembly proposes that the draft law amending the Law on the Prevention of Employment and Employment should be adopted in a black text. ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u5	Thank you, Madam Vice President, for the presentation. With this, honourable Members, we are moving on to a presentation of the views of the groups of Members. First of all, Mr Sonibo Knezak, the Group of Social Democrats. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u6	Thank you, President. Dear colleagues! The Group of Social Democrats supports the Government and the competent ministry's actions, which contribute to the reduction of work in black. In the past, we have already supported systemic measures that included increased control of inspection services and incentives for employers and workers to carry out their work in legal forms, contributing, of course, their share of taxes and contributions to the budget, which provides money for public investment and services and social security for all citizens and citizens of the Republic of Slovenia. As an incentive for individuals performing personal complementary work, we have also supported the introduction of a valuation. The proposed Act on Amendments to the Law on Prevention of Work and Black Employment introduces only one substantive amendment, which is necessary in order to bring the law into line with the financial legislation, which is being amended in order to implement the European Payment Services Directive in the internal market. By adopting the directive, the European Union prohibited paymentees from charging fees and commissions for non-cash payments made by consumers using a payment or credit card. The Law on the Prevention of Work and Employment provides in black that the costs of payment of social contributions must be borne by the insurer. The law also allows an individual to pay social contributions for the performance of personal supplementary work with a valuer, using a payment card through an electronic system provided by the Slovenian Public Payments Authority. So far, the law has stipulated that the cost of paying the value for personal supplementary work with the payment card must be borne by an individual. With this amendment of the law, we abolish the charging of these fees and commissions to individuals who pay social contributions for personal supplementary work with a valuer online. We will support the proposed amendments to the law by removing the cost of paying the value over the Internet, which will further encourage individuals performing personal complementary work to carry out the complementary work and, at the final stage, also to pay social contributions.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u11	Thank you, Madam Members, for presenting your position. Mr Vojko Starović, you have the floor on behalf of the Allanka Bratušek Group, please.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u12	Thank you for your word, chairman. Your Honor! The purpose of the amendment of the Law on the Prevention of Labour and on Employment is to harmonise national legislation with the Directive of the European Union. Despite the fact that the intervention in labour and employment legislation is black-handed, it is right to address the problems of the grey economy. For a large part of it is also work and employment in black. Black work is the phenomenon of a complex nature, which is difficult to define by a single definition. The main causes of this phenomenon are improvements in the social situation of individuals, lower labour costs, avoidance of taxes, increased unemployment rates and also a decline in confidence in state institutions. The consequences of work and employment in black are not negligible, as this affects all people. Firstly, the State loses budgetary resources at the expense of those individuals who do not contribute to the state treasury, from which social and health services, pensions and other services are then financed in the public interest. Secondly: persons who carry out their activities lawfully must contribute so much more to the cash flow of funds that the foundations of the social state can be maintained at all. The negative consequences of employment and black work are also felt by those who do black work as complementary activities. In this way they create the possibility of losing their legal employment, because of the additional burden they often get sick and from behind we have the disease stock. Those persons who perform black work as the only form of employment are neither insured nor legally protected, not receiving paid absences due to the hospital stock, and employers do not comply with the rules laid down in the Systemic Labour Law. Such workers are often further exploited. Most often, weaker groups of individuals are involved in the work, these are unemployed, self-employed, and students. Why? For black market employers, it means lower prices and faster services, while for workers it would not be otherwise profitable. So black work is a problem that all countries face. However, it is not possible to attribute the blame exclusively to the State. Much depends on the general awareness of people and the individual’s attitude toward the problem. For all these reasons, we will support the proposal to amend the law in the SAB Group. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u17	Thank you, Member, for your presentation. As a last resort, Mrs Nina Maurovich, Group of Ambassadors of the List of Marjan Šarc. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u18	Thank you so much for saying that. Respected and respected! Today we are discussing the proposal for a law amending the Law on the Prevention of Employment and Work in the Black. Although this time the bill only deals with minimum amendments based on the harmonisation of the rules in our legislation, it is still important to stress the importance of this law. Throughout time, all countries of the world, a gray economy that has become part of the lives of individuals has spread in silence and at first almost undetected between us. The grey economy and work in black appeared as a virtual rescue plant to unemployed individuals and also to individuals whose income was not sufficient to survive and thus spread to all times of today’s society. The rise in work and employment in black typically occurs at a time of economic crisis, as at that time, with low incomes and increasing inflation in living needs, people are serving a number of jobs that provide them with additional earnings. Work and employment in black have some definitions. The International Labour Organisation has first defined employment in black as a job that is not in accordance with the requirements of national law, regulation and legal practice. In Slovenia, we have work on the black law defined in the law that we are considering today. According to the law, employment is black when the employer and the worker enters into a contract of employment or another type of contract which is the basis for the performance of the work but does not apply for health and pension and disability insurance. Employment in black is also when the employer employs a person without citizenship and without legally established permits. Such problems are being faced by countries around the world, as work and employment are practically everywhere. The main difference between countries is that each country faces this problem separately and in different ways, as it also depends on the cause. The very prevention of work in black play a significant role by the State as legislator and supervisor. That work and employment in black is well regulated in the country is important, since it affects all people in the country, despite the fact that many are unaware of its consequences. The important consequences of such work and employment are fewer jobs and jobs, fewer opportunities for entrepreneurs, work without social security, the deterioration of pension and health allowance, tax evasion and contributions, and so on. It should also be stressed that social and other services of public interest, such as education, health care, maternity, child support and so on, are financed from contributions to public treasury. There are no contributions to the state treasury in the case of black-and-black employment, so the other employees must contribute so much more to it that the foundations of the social states are preserved and maintained. However, work in black has no consequences only on the state budget, but also on workers, because they do not have compulsory health insurance, are not insured in case of accidents at work, the working conditions are unsupervised and often worse and are subject to a higher risk of loss of work, are without the right to a retirement, annual leave, regression, regression for food, reimbursement of travel costs, and work done is not taken into account for working time and is not taken into account for retirement. For all the reasons set out above, we believe that the country must strive for as low a level of work and employment as possible in order to improve the working conditions and care for citizens. The law we have before us serves to limit it, and any additions to this law, which are further improving it, are welcome. That is why we will support the proposal.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u21	Thank you, Member, for the additional explanation. The competent parent working body is the Committee on Infrastructure, Environment and Space. I'm handing the word to the vice president of mags. Andrey Reich. Here you go, Mr. Ambassador.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u22	Thank you very much, Mr. President. All in attendance, all in attendance! At its 9th meeting, on 5 June, the Committee on Infrastructure, Environment and Space considered the draft Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Drivers Act submitted by a group of Members and Members with the first-signed Boris Doblekar and Janjo Slug to the National Assembly for consideration on a regular basis. On behalf of the proposer, Mrs Janja Slug and Mr Doblekar stressed that the key objective of the proposed law is to enable voluntary firefighting units to be more operational in the event of accidents. The draft law will thus allow smaller C1 fire-fighting vehicles and smaller Category B fire-fighting vehicles to be operated by operational fire-extinguishers who will have a driving licence for category B motor vehicles and a valid certificate of competence for driving these vehicles. This exemption would apply only for the duration of the fire service and only in the territory of the Republic of Slovenia, unless it enters into bilateral agreements with countries where similar exceptions apply, such as Austria or Germany, for example. The proposed exemption will thus improve the operational readiness of firefighters under the condition that the competent ministry accurately prescribes a training programme, which, following the example of the Slovenian Armed Forces and in accordance with the direction of the Slovenian Armed Forces, should be strictly adapted to the needs of firefighters within the competence of the Protection and Rescue Board and its existing records under the Vulcan Information System. Due to the principle of equality before the law, the same exception shall be applied to both voluntary and professional firefighters, rescue and special ambulance drivers for the implementation of emergency medical assistance and intervention vehicles of civil protection, mountain, cave and underwater rescue services at the level of the State and mobile units of the ecological laboratory whose maximum permissible mass exceeds 3,500 kilograms. State Secretary, Ministry of Infrastructure Mag. Nina Mauhler stressed that the Government supports the fundamental objective pursued by the proposal for a law. With regard to the solutions that provide for exceptions to the use of smaller fire vehicles, ambulances and civil protection intervention vehicles, it suggests that police vehicles intended to carry out police operational tasks are included as exceptions. With regard to the proposed solution for the Protection and Rescue Administration to implement the driver training and verification programme for driving those motor vehicles, the Government's opinion that the proposal for a law would have implications for the State budget and other public financial resources, since it does not have sufficient expertise to carry out these tasks. There are also currently no organisational and spatial conditions provided for this purpose. The National Council for Local Self-Government and Regional Development, as well as the Gasilian Commitment of Slovenia, also supported the proposal. In the short discussion, the discussion was supported by the proposal for a law, and disagreement was expressed with the Government's proposal that the selection process of the driver's training programme provider and the examination of driver's skills should be led by the Public Transport Safety Agency and that such education through this agency should be taken over by schools of driving which do not own such vehicles. It was also pointed out that training would not be entirely free of charge and the cost of the country, but costs would be inadequately lower than those for obtaining a C1 driving licence. In this regard, there will also be no major financial implications for the state budget. Following the discussion, the Committee adopted all the amendments tabled by the proposed groups of Members of the draft law and, in accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, voted on and adopted all the articles of the draft law together. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u26	Thank you, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen, honourable Members! You have before you the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Energy Law. The adoption of this legal novelty is important both for the country and for citizens as well as for final consumers of electricity and natural gas. There are several reasons for the adoption of the novelty, and it is particularly important at this time that the European Commission of Slovenia sees the incorrect transposition of the two directives, namely the Energy Efficiency Directive of buildings and the Energy Efficiency Directive. That's exactly what we're doing with this novel. It is also necessary to harmonise the law in the light of the 2016 Constitutional Court decision and in order to comply with the Guidelines on State aid for the protection of the environment and energy for the period 2014-2020. In addition, the proposal also contains other important changes that address the shortcomings that have emerged in the implementation of the current Energy Act, or the anomalies that need to be addressed both for the benefit of the market and for the benefit of customers and other players in the energy market. The proposal also provides the legal basis for a wide range of improvements to existing systems. The law before you today contains provisions that the Government believes are good and professionally justified. Allow me to brief some of them in the introduction. In the field of electricity and natural gas, the concept of regular prices is deleted, allowing the Energy Agency to conduct a comparison of all suppliers' prices and not just regular prices as set out now. It is explicitly pointed out that the misleading and unfair way of selling electricity and natural gas is also prohibited through intermediaries, thus encouraging suppliers to pursue a fair way of selling. An appropriate legal basis for the processing of personal data in the Advanced Measurement System is added and the law is aligned with the GDPR Regulation with the relevant legal basis. An appropriate legal basis is provided for the adoption of a regulation by which the Government of the Republic of Slovenia will determine the type of electricity generation equipment from renewable energy sources and cogeneration of heat and electricity for which no building permit is required. An appropriate legal basis for self-sustainment of electricity produced from renewable energy sources is laid down. Although self-sustainment has already been under way since the beginning of 2016 under the Regulation on self-sustainment of electricity from renewable energy sources, the existing regulation has not been sufficiently clear and, above all, has not been ambitious enough, so the Government, by adopting a new regulation this March, has improved this measure and has also allowed self-sustainment for citizens who have not had these options so far. These content also needs to be regulated at the legal level. A range of eligible applicants for calls by the Energy Agency for the allocation of funds under the renewable energy support scheme shall be expanded to take advantage of the full potential of companies placing installations on renewable energy sources in such a way that they can also run at the Agency's call for projects they intend to implement in cooperation with investors. The proposal follows European trends in the transition to a low-carbon society and represents an important step towards coherence with the European acquis and the active integration of environmentally-aware customers into this process. We believe that the proposal for a novelty for citizens, the environment and the energy sector brings much good, and I also hope for your equal knowledge and support. Finally: The Government of the Republic of Slovenia, on the basis of Article 138 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, proposes to the National Assembly to hold a third consideration of the draft law at the same meeting. Thank you very much.

In view of the fact that in these cases it is a method of production which places a heavy burden on the environment, they consider that this provision contained in the proposal should be deleted. However, by the SDS Group, a general assessment was made that there is a consensus in Slovenia that maximum efforts should be made to invest in obtaining as much electricity from renewable sources as possible, but this raises the question of how these objectives will be achieved if the Government leaves any possibility of building a hydroelectric power plant on the Mura River at the same time. The belief was expressed that wind and solar energy targets in the area of renewable energy would certainly not be achieved. ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u46	Thank you for your word. I cannot agree with a colleague who said today that every user wants only the cheapest position and that he will never run out of electricity. Why can't I actually agree with my colleague? I very much welcome myself and advocate that more electricity be from renewable sources. But to return to the darkness today. The main reason for the adoption of the Energy Law is the need to adapt to the European right order, to implement the Constitutional Court decision and to comply with the guidelines on state aid for the environment and energy. In addition to these amendments, some additional provisions have been introduced in the draft law, but they are not necessary. One of these is the change in the definition of the certificate of origin. The current legislation defines a certificate of origin as a public document in electronic form proving that a certain amount of electricity is produced in a high-efficiency cogeneration or from renewable energy sources, and the proposal of the government novelty extends this definition. The Government proposes that certificates of origin may be issued not only for electricity produced from renewable energy sources but also from other conventional sources such as hydropower, nuclear power plants, thermal power plants and the like. The certificate allows for transparent monitoring and proof of the origin of electricity and, consequently, will allow greater market transparency. Its main purpose is to facilitate electricity trading produced from renewable sources of energy or cogeneration, and in particular to improve transparency on the electricity market. The proposer or the government allows consumers to choose from which source to buy electricity. It also provides the legal basis and the obligation for distributors to implement this provision. ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u60	Your Honor! Today's draft law covers the amendment of the three articles of the current Law on intangible securities and adds another completely new article. From a nomo technical point of view, there are minor changes to the current law, but they are still very important in substance. In Article 27 of the current law, it is a minor amendment that will simplify, facilitate, accelerate and reduce the enforcement of a legal decision on the operation of the heirs. The members of the Clearing House of Deposits will, however, be allowed to harmonise the enforcement of the decisions on succession. The other solutions relate to the closure of the registration account with KDD and to the enforcement of the decision of the Constitutional Court, by which it annulled the first and second orders of Article 48, paragraph 4, or the abolition of the limitation on the charge of the annual cost of keeping a security account for natural persons. The Law on intangibleised securities, which was adopted by the National Assembly in autumn 2015, abolished registration accounts with KDD for natural persons with effect from 2017. The termination process was carried out as part of preparations for integration into the TARGET2-Securities European settlement platform. The registration account was set up for purposes of privatisation and allowed the distribution of non-materialised shares. However, since it was not run by a stock exchange company, it was not in line with the standards of the European platform. The law provided for the holders of securities which had not been transferred to trading accounts in stock exchanges companies to be used under the Statute of the Court of First Instance, which had not come to life. The data show that, after two years of official withdrawal, KDD still accounts for approximately 80 000 registered accounts with the remaining privatisation securities. The new law regulates the issue of the forgotten property by keeping it in a joint account with KDD for two years and then transferring it to Kad. The transfer to court is therefore no longer envisaged. The proposed regulation improves the situation for all stakeholders. With this arrangement, the court would not be burdened by thousands of proceedings and the holders of securities in cancelled registration accounts could still take them over a period of two years. This reduces and evaluates procedures in contrast to existing legislation. In the event that the owners did not decide to transfer to the stock exchange company during that period, the securities would then be transferred to a special account with Kad. This would result in the acquisition by the State or, in particular, the pensioners, since the securities abandoned could also be used to contribute Kad to the pension fund. With regard to the enforcement of the Constitutional Court decision, however, it should be noted that, due to the criticism of the costs, the National Assembly has limited those costs to keeping the account dependent on the value of the property on it, but, at the request of the companies for constitutional assessment, the Constitutional Court has repealed that provision. They repealed the Article which limited the costs of the individual’s commercial account, since the National Assembly did not demonstrate the existence of a public advantage to limit these costs. This amendment to the law is thus proof that the simplest solutions without needless design of complex systemic solutions and populism contributions on the protection of small shareholders are the most effective, cheap and for the benefit of all stakeholders involved. Therefore, we hope that the present story of the abolition of registration accounts will be a lesson for the future. On the basis of these reasons, we will support the proposal for a law in the Group of the Modern Centre Party. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u62	Thank you for your word and a nice hello! The New Law on Intangible Securities seeks to correct, solve problems arising from the adverse decisions of the past government and the adverse decision of the Constitutional Court, if I go back a little to history. This law, the Law on Intangible Securities, was passed by Cerara in 2015. At that time the TARGET2 payment system was transferred into our legislation in accordance with the requirements of the European Union, but at the same time they added one more thing to this law, which had no link to the European Union directives. This is the cancellation of the registration accounts. The registration accounts were invoices held by the majority of people in middle years or slightly older who received these shares during the transition through the distribution of shares. They were not traded and kept, stored on the KDD Clearing Company. In 2015, 280 000 registration accounts were still active or opened. I repeat, they were people who weren't any stockbrokers or anything like that. It's the people who got the shares during the transition and saved them at the Clearing House. However, this amendment of the law is somewhat under the pretext that the European Union is asking us to do so, although there was no such requirement in this TARGET2 payment system, this amendment required that these register accounts, which were free of charge at the Clearing House of Deposits, be closed and that their owners must have their assets, these few shares which they had, transferred to the stock exchange houses and opened commercial accounts there, which of course they did. The opening costs were around EUR 20, 25, 30 and then they pay commissions every year. The problem is that of these 280 thousand registration accounts, most of them were very small, I don't know 50, 100 euros and so on, and that's why in Levica and then the right-wing opposition, we strongly criticised this solution because it actually led to the mass decommissioning of 280 thousand small shareholders who had some funds stored at the Clearing House. Our criticisms were carried out, but in the end a provision was adopted that incorporated at least some minimum protection for these shareholders and protected against their property being easily seized by a stock exchange house if you multiply 280 thousand times 50 — that is no longer so little money once you concentrate it. In particular, this provision was that a broker may charge up to half a percentage of the value of shares for the operation of a commercial account. This was after, I think, more than one year of experimentation then finally adopted, but we regret that the Constitutional Court has now ruled that this violates the constitutional principle of a free economic initiative; in short, the decision goes to the stock exchange houses of the financial industry and this is also the main amendment of this law. Thus, the lifting of this restriction and the reinstatement or application of what was the original purpose of this imposed provision in the law is that the stock exchange houses can basically decommission these small shareholders within a few years. That's why we can't support this marriage. However, this law also has some positive things; for example, one is that the securities in the cancelled register accounts of those who have not been transferred to the stock exchange houses are then placed at the courts, but we have already warned that the courts will be overloaded. Well, now we've come up with the right solution, that's to transfer it to a joint trust account run by the Clearing House. This will burden the courts administratively, which is why we will not vote against the law, but we will abstain. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u64	Thank you very much, Madam Vice President. Dear colleagues and colleagues, distinguished representatives of the government! We're talking about intangible paper values. In fact, it is a statement by the issuer entered in the central register of intangible securities by which the issuer undertakes to comply with the obligation contained in the securities. The holder of the intangible securities shall be the person for whom the non-materialised securities are entered in the central register. The rights of intangible securities shall be exercised through the Clearing House of Deputies and the KDD system members in accordance with internationally established standards for the performance of corporate acts. The proposal for the amendment of the Law on intangible securities brings three significant changes. First time. Entry of securities transfer orders on the basis of decisions on inheritance into the register. The current regime provides, therefore, that orders for the transfer of intangible securities based on decisions or decisions of public authorities, such as succession decisions, shall be entered into the central register by a central depository company, unless the decision requires the conduct of a KDD member, such as a commercial bank. The draft law that we are considering in implementing the decisions on succession to the Central Register only removes the restrictions on implementation to the KDD, which means that members of KDD, such as, for example, once again, banks, will be able to implement the decision on succession for domestic and foreign securities themselves. This is supposed to simplify the procedure for heirs, and we welcome that. The submission of orders for the transfer of non-materialised securities to the central register on the basis of all other decisions of the public authorities, such as enforcement or insurance decisions, remains unchanged. Second solution, second change. Transfer of intangible securities from cancelled registry accounts. The proposal provides that non-materialised securities in cancelled registrar accounts shall be transferred instead of to the competent court's account, the court's case, to a joint account maintained by the KDD. This should prevent a major administrative burden on the courts and, indeed, if the administrative burden of the courts is to be reduced. That's what we welcome. Persons whose non-materialised securities have been transferred to a common account may, by 31 December 2021, require those securities to be transferred to an account with a KDD member. After that day, after 31. 12. 2021, these securities shall be transferred to a special account at Kad. The third solution, compliance with the constitutional decision, is essentially a logical solution. The law provided that the annual cost of keeping the account and the compensation for maintaining the account held by a member of the central depository company for a natural person should not exceed 0,5 % of the average value of the intangible securities in that account. However, the law authorises the KDD, hereinafter referred to as this paragraph, to reduce this limit further. In view of the fact that the Constitutional Court repealed the limit of 0,5 %, this provision on the possibility of a reduction is also irrelevant and is deleted by the proposal for a law. The paragraph only provides that members of the KDD shall not charge additional costs for opening and closing the account, except for the costs set by the daily tariff. So, under the line; both the first and the second solutions reduce administrative burdens, the third amendment is the logical consequence of the decision of the Constitutional Court and therefore the novel Nova Slovenija supports. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u65	Thank you very much. Mr Franz Kramar will present the position of the SAB Group. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u66	Madam Vice President, distinguished colleagues and colleagues, representatives of the Government! It is not unusual for the implementation of a specific legal provision in practice to prove to be inoperable. As a result, we expect a response from the legislator towards a different regulation of a specific provision. In the Parliamentary Group of Alenka Bratušek we advocate the removal of all unnecessary administrative obstacles, to simplify the procedures through which citizens exercise their rights and burden of justice. However, in the case of such changes, we are always weighing whether a different, simplified regulation deregulations a certain legal regime and endangers legal certainty. Two of these proposed amendments and the addition of the existing Non-Materialised Securities Act follow the said burdens of citizens and the judiciary and the third amendment responds to the constitutional decision. The first amendment concerns the issue of transfer of securities on the basis of a legal decision on succession. Under the current regime, the heir must, on the basis of one decision on inheritance, require transfer both to the KDD and to domestic securities as to a member of KDD for foreign securities, even though the defector had all the securities in the account with the KDD member. Since such arrangements are both time-consuming and cost-optimal, the proposed amendment provides for a solution whereby the transfer of domestic and foreign securities will be fully implemented by a KDD member. Until the start of the registration account decommissioning process, most of the securities accounts were registered and could therefore only be led by a KDD member. Over time, the process of decommissioning of register accounts has expired, but a further concentration of entries has been maintained on the basis of decisions of the national authorities at the KDD. Taking into account these changes in the decommissioning of register accounts, we have seen the proposed solution in the Alenka Bratušek Party as appropriate, as it does not jeopardise the legal security of citizens and citizens. At the same time, the heirs are provided with a simpler, simpler and cheaper procedure and members of the KDD with a comprehensive enforcement of a specific decision on inheritance. In the Alenka Bratušek Party, we would like to point out that this amendment applies only to the decision on succession and not to other decisions of the State authorities, such as the enforcement decision, the insurance decision and the like. In order for the same regime to apply to specifically exposed decisions, it is necessary to amend the sectoral legislation, namely the Law on Enforcement and Insurance. As we advocate a unified regulation of a specific legal issue, we expect amendments from the competent ministry in this direction as well. The second amendment simplifies the transfer of securities from cancelled registration accounts to KDD. The registration accounts for KDD were established for the purposes of the privatisation exercise, as it was otherwise not possible to distribute shares. Such registration accounts represented only a temporary solution, i.e. until the shareholders took over the shares. Since the shareholders of these securities have not taken over or transferred them to the accounts of individual members, the law provides that the securities not transferred are transferred to the court’s deposit. However, the Court’s deposit is a judicial proceeding that takes place in a court of law and constitutes a certain level of burden. This turned out to be unnecessary and, secondly, to a beneficiary who you do not want to bear the costs of such a procedure. The arrangements are also inadequate because the courts opened accounts with KDD for the purposes of the Court’s deposit, not with its members. Therefore, it is not possible to exercise corporate rights under KDD securities. In view of the fact that the beneficiaries have not taken over the securities either from court deposits, the law proposes that all the securities not transferred are transferred to the common dedicated account with KDD. If they are not taken over by the beneficiaries by 31 December 2021, the securities shall be transferred to a special account with Kad. They shall be transferred together with all the yields, payments and cash benefits received by KDD from the specific securities. Even if the financial buy-in is not covered by this title, in the Alenka Bratušek Party we consider that they should be used for the benefit of all citizens and citizens, for example, for the purposes of the demographic fund. We therefore support the proposed legislative change. The last, so the third amendment refers to the abolition of the unconstitutional nature of the part of Article 48(4). This provides that the annual cost of keeping the account and maintaining the account held by a KDD member for a natural person shall not exceed 0,5 % of the average value of the share in that account. Given that the tariff is legally fixed and that the refunds must be consistent with reasonable business conditions and non-discriminatory, we also support this proposal, which is now constitutionally compatible.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u67	Thank you very much. The next Mr Robert Polnar will present the position of the Desus Group.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u68	Madam Vice-President, Members of Parliament and Members of Parliament! The most important objectives of the proposal for a law amending the Law on intangible securities are to simplify and accelerate transfers of securities relating to their succession and transfer from cancelled registration accounts to a special account. The basic law was adopted in October 2015. The whole new legal text had to be adopted for the purpose of establishing a project of the T2S Eurosystem aimed at setting up a single technical basis for securities settlement between central deposit firms and the central bank for the settlement of monetary work. The objectives of the Member States of the European Union that have integrated into this system are to say that the T2S system, including Slovenia, are to achieve greater efficiency and security of the implementation of securities settlement, to unify domestic and cross-border settlement procedures and to reduce existing differences in the settlement costs of domestic and cross-border transactions. The expected consequences are two; in particular, to allow easier access for investors to securities issued in other central depository companies and securities issuers to improve the possibility of expanding the international investor base. This is important for the development of the capital market in Slovenia, since it increases its liquidity and enables new ways of obtaining financial resources for companies, for their development and business. The closure of the registration accounts of the central depository company was established from 1 January 2017 and was a result of the inclusion in the T2S system, as the registration accounts are not in accordance with its standards. The registration accounts were created as a result of the privatisation and distribution of shares in such a way that the shareholder was not in a legal relationship with the member of the central depository company. To this end, individual owners had to transfer securities to stock exchanges until 1 January 2017. In the event that they did not do so, it was intended to transfer the funds to a court deposit. The estimated value of all securities before transfer was 7 billion and 700 million euros, outstanding for 67 million euros, is said to be a poor percentage of the basic value. The owners of these accounts are supposed to be about 80 grand, according to the latest data. In view of the legal requirement for the transfer of securities from a central depository company to a court deposit, the Court should make several thousand decisions, which would constitute a complex, delayed and cost-effective operation. There is no doubt that the courts could not have done what was prescribed by the law. The securities were not transferred to a court deposit and remained at the central depository. Today, we are resolving the open case in a way that, by amending the law, provides for the transfer of those securities from the central depository company that have not yet been transferred to the stock exchange houses directly to the Capital Society. The capital company will act as the custodian of these securities until 31 December 2021, after which it will become the owner of these securities. In the Desus Group, we support solutions that streamline the process of completing ownership. It is acceptable for us that after 2021 the Capital Company will own and manage abandoned securities. The capital company will transform into a demographic reserve fund in the future and it is important that the fund has as many sources of funding as possible. We are realists, so we know that the source of funding from abandoned securities will be of a more marginal nature, but surely not to the extent that we can just overlook it or simply shake hands with it. In the Desus Group we will support the proposal for a law amending the Law on intangible securities. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u74	Dear President, distinguished representatives of the Ministry, colleague and colleague! However, in its decision, the Constitutional Court considered that such an arrangement constitutes an infringement of the right to a constitutionally guaranteed free economic initiative, since the relative limitation of costs constitutes a legislative restriction on contractual freedom in relation to the essential component of the contractual relationship, that is to say the price. Years ago, the National Assembly adopted an amendment restricting the charge for keeping the account up. Which meant that these brokerage houses by keeping portfolios to a certain value actually created a minus. Therefore, they became, if we can say, creative and charged additional services of questionable nature. The National Assembly then considered even the authentic interpretation of this article; in short, what some of the treatments were needed in this house because of this senseless intervention. Finally, the Constitutional Court annulled this. In addition, it is provided that intangible securities are transferred to a joint account maintained by the Clearing House of Deposits in closed registry accounts rather than to the account of the competent court. Persons whose non-materialised securities have been transferred to a common account may require that such securities be transferred to an account with a KDD member by 31 December 2021. After that date, they shall be transferred to a special account of the Capital Society, i.e. to the future demographic fund. ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u75	Thank you very much. Mr Jani Prednik will be the last to present the position of the SD Group. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u76	Dear representatives of the government, colleagues and colleagues, good afternoon! Amendments to the law proposed by the Government are a matter of continuing in the previous term the process of lifting so-called registration accounts with the Clearing House of Deputies. In the 1990s, privatisation certificates were placed on registration accounts managed by KDD and were used only for the registration or ownership of these securities, and not for trading them. After almost two decades of continuous harmonisation of our legislation with the European Financial Directives, the registration accounts were abolished in the previous term by adopting a new Securities Act. At the time of its adoption, it was decided to cancel the registration accounts. Securities holders had two options; first, the transfer of securities to a trading account at a stock exchange house, which resulted in higher costs. Second of all, securities waiver. If the holders do not transfer them to commercial accounts by 2021, these securities shall be transferred to Kad ownership, which will become a demographic fund. The law stipulated that untransferable papers shall be kept as court deposits. This arrangement has proved to be inappropriate over time, with some 80,000 holders remaining undeclared for both the transfer to commercial accounts and the express suspension, which suggests that they will probably not do so until 2021 and that the securities will in any event come into Kad's possession. Otherwise, this is mainly the number of procedures; of the total value of securities, about 7,7 billion remained in the register accounts for 67 million, but are distributed among 80,000 different holders. In order to complete the closure of the register accounts, the bill offers some new solutions. An order for the transfer of securities arising from the decision of the authority will also be open to a stock exchange house, the so-called KDD member, not only to KDD. This shall accelerate procedures and facilitate the continuation of the procedure to heirs of securities. The securities not declared by the owners have so far been deposited with the Court of Justice as a deposit for each individual holder, representing a large number of proceedings. A common dedicated account is therefore being introduced, within which uniform procedures can be managed, which will be substantially more transparent and also cheaper. By the end of 2021, owners will still be able to request the transfer of their securities to a commercial account, as provided for by the original law, and thus will not lose the opportunity to have their property at their disposal. At the time of adoption of the law in the previous term, the most powder was raised by the amendments adopted to Article 48, which limited the costs to be charged indirectly by stock exchanges to their customers for the use of commercial accounts. This restriction has been annulled by the Constitutional Court despite accepted authentic explanations as an unacceptable intervention in the free economic initiative and the freedom to conclude contracts. Therefore, Article 3 of this proposal aligns the law with the Constitutional Court’s decision by deleting the deleted sentence and it is clearly stated in the law that brokers can only charge customers the costs laid down by their tariffs but not the additional costs for opening and closing the account. In this way, at least part of the possibility for stockbrokers to charge their customers excessive costs is limited. The Legislative and Legal Service has pointed out some of the shortcomings of the law that have been lifted by the amendments adopted. In the SD Group of Members, we will support the bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u80	Honourable Vice-President of the National Assembly, honourable Members of the National Assembly! Allow me to begin by apologizing to the Foreign Minister, Dr Cerar, who is today at the General Affairs Council of the European Union in Luxembourg, and therefore cannot attend this meeting. The question of the proposed amendment to the current procedure for the appointment of a candidate, candidate for the European Commissioner or Commissioner from Slovenia was addressed by the Government at its meeting on 13 June this year. On the basis of the harmonised opinion of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and the Office of the Government of Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Cooperation between the National Assembly and the Government in the case of the European Union of 9 May, the Commission adopted a negative position. In accordance with the provisions in force referred to in Article 11b of the Law on the Proposal for a Candidate or Candidate for a European Commissioner or Commissioner from Slovenia, the Government shall decide at a regular meeting. He's making his proposal to the National Assembly. The proposed candidate or candidate shall then be represented at the working body of the National Assembly responsible for European affairs, which shall vote on or give a reasoned opinion on it. The Government is not bound by this opinion, but before submitting the proposal to the competent institutions of the European Union, it must inform and explain the proposal. In the Government, we believe that such a selection procedure is appropriate. It was established in 2015 in response to the problems that have been demonstrated in the implementation of the pre-election procedure. It filled the legal void and legalized a comparatively appropriate form of cooperation between the Government and the National Assembly in this process, which does not suggest a change before it was first tested in practice. The current regulation, on the one hand, provides for a democratic impact, since the competent Committee on European Affairs, to which European Members from Slovenia may also be invited, votes on the candidate or candidate and gives a reasoned opinion. On the other hand, it allows the Government the appropriate flexibility it needs in negotiations with the Commissioner for the composition of the European Commission, both in terms of personnel composition and in terms of the allocation of competences for specific areas between Commissioners and Commissioners. The proposed amendment to the law whereby the Government should obtain the written consent of most European Members from Slovenia before adopting a candidate or candidate for a member or member of the European Commission, in our opinion, is not in accordance with the applicable legislative regime. The government is an independent body of the executive branch of the State, representing Slovenia as a legal person both at home and in the international community. It shall be responsible for its work and decisions to the National Assembly, which shall also inform it of its decisions within the framework of the European Union, to which Slovenia has transferred, in accordance with Article 3a of the Constitution, part of the exercise of sovereign rights. Such a constitutional regulation cannot be bound by the law of the exercise of individual government powers, including the human resources function, to the written consent of most Slovenian Members in the European Parliament. These are not part of the system of division of power in Slovenia, so the decision of the competent authority of our country cannot depend on their consent. This would, in our view, be contrary to the principles of the division of powers of the rule of law and the independence of the Government in the performance of its functions, which are the basis of constitutional regulation. Such a solution is also not supported in comparative law, as none of the members of the European Union know it. The Government also assesses that the amendment proposal does not comply with the Treaty of the European Union, which regulates the procedure for appointing members of the European Commission in Article 17. This Article provides that the selection of the European Commissioner or Commissioner shall be carried out by the Council of the EU, which shall, in agreement with the President-elect of the European Commission, adopt a list of persons proposed for appointment as members of the Commission. The proposed composition of the Commission is therefore within the limits of the Council in which the governments of the Member States are represented. Thus, as directly elected members of the European Parliament, MEPs are not included in the proposing phase for members of the Commission College. The implementation of the proposal would be contrary to the current domestic and European legal order, could lead to stagnation in the process of setting up the European Commission, could affect the existing interinstitutional balance as set out in the Treaty on European Union, would not contribute to strengthening the role of the European Commission as an independent guardian of the core treaties, but would have opened up a space of politicisation at the stage of its creation, which would not be in the interest of Slovenia and the EU as a whole. Given the Government's proposal that the National Assembly not support the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Law on cooperation between the National Assembly and the Government in EU matters. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u86	Thank you very much, Madam Vice President. Dear guests, fellow Members and Members! Moreover, the proposal would reach an interinstitutional balance as set out in Article 17 of the Treaty of the European Union. The appointment of a member and a member of the European Commission in all Member States of the European Union is the responsibility of the Government. Governments draw on their legitimacy in individual national parliaments, and MEPs are not directly involved in the proposing phase of the Commission. However, it is true that each country can make its own decision and have a different procedure. Following the current procedure for the application of a candidate for a member of the European Commission, the Government shall decide at a regular meeting which shall forward its proposal to the National Assembly. As usual, the candidate shall be presented on the committee responsible and then the parent working body shall vote on it and shall send an explanation to the Government. This means that the existing procedure provides sufficient manoeuvre for the National Assembly to decide on the candidate's proposal and allows the Government sufficient flexibility for final decision-making. We believe that the existing procedure does not have a democratic deficit, as the proposaler claims, but is, in our view, open and transparent enough. ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u88	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Dear Secretary of State with fellow Members, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! A group of first-signed members, Joseph Horvat, tabled a bill a few weeks before the European elections, which they wish, if I may say so, to put the system of appointing a Commissioner from Slovenia in the European Union on the table. The current arrangements have been implemented 30. 4. 2015 without a vote against it, so Nova Slovenija did not object. At that time, it was stipulated that the Government would propose a candidate for a member of the European Commission on the basis of a notification from the competent EU institutions and would forward the proposal to the National Assembly. The Government shall decide on the proposal at its regular session. The candidate shall present himself to the working body of the National Assembly responsible for European affairs, which shall vote on and deliver an opinion on the candidate by way of an explanation and shall send it to the Government without delay. Thus, the Government has in principle the support or non-support of representatives of the National Assembly. The arrangement makes sense from the point of view of the division of power between the Government and the National Assembly. At the time, there was no other solution to the current solutions that showed that the current regime was not the best in terms of the division of power between the Government and the National Assembly. A solution is now proposed to obtain the written consent of a majority, i.e. at least five Members from the Republic of Slovenia in the European Parliament, before accepting a candidate or candidate for a member or member of the European Commission. Such a solution is contrary to the Constitution, since the Government is accountable to the National Assembly. The government also represents Slovenia in the international community on the basis of the Government Act. It shall inform the National Assembly of its decisions, which may take decisions that the Government shall take into account in its functioning. Thus, for example, all matters of the European Union shall be dealt with in the National Assembly at which the National Assembly adopts its views. As a result, in the Desus Group, we see no point in the proposal to share power between the Government and the National Assembly, even with Slovenian Members in the European Parliament, these are not part of the power in the Republic of Slovenia. Certainly in the Desus Group we see the proposed solution as the possibility of blocking the appointment of the European Commissioner by the candidates and the desire to influence the appointment of the candidate from Slovenia. It should also be noted that the solution is contrary to the Treaty on European Union, which provides for the appointment of the Council of the European Union in agreement with the President of the European Commission. In the Desus Group, we believe that elected Members in the European Parliament should work for what they have been elected to do. One of these powers of the European Parliament is the appointment of the President of the European Commission and Commissioners and the statement of non-trust to the European Commission as a whole. Thus, the application for appointment by Members of the European Union is clear because they represent the legislative authority. For all of this, the Desus Group will vote against the fact that the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on cooperation between the National Assembly and the Government in matters of the European Union is appropriate for further consideration. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u92	Thank you very much, Madam Vice President. Dear representatives of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, colleagues and fellow Members, welcome to all! The European Union was planned as a community after World War II as an element of peace and cooperation. It began with Schuman's declaration and its functioning and its institutions changed significantly over the years, some gained and some lost their powers. The most powerful institutions are the European Parliament, the European Council, the Council of Ministers and, of course, the European Commission. The European Commission was set up in 1958 as an apolitical instrument, but has since evolved into a political body whose function is primarily enforceable; the body implementing the European Union's budgetary policies is a co-legislator and represents the European Union as well as in the international field. We have a period ahead of us when a new composition of the European Commission will take place. The decision for the President or President of the Commission and its members will be so much more important because in the autumn decisions such as brexit, the establishment of a multi-annual financial framework for the period 2020-2027 and many others await us. For the Republic of Slovenia, it will be particularly important to establish funding for the multiannual financial framework, especially in terms of our agriculture and cohesion policy. In addition, a reform of the European Union will be needed, leading to its more successful and effective functioning. Another extremely important thing and a point that we are devoting too little attention to both Slovenia and the European Union is the future. The future, based on the values of the rule of law, the protection of human rights, adequate social security and a safe and healthy environment with modern infrastructure, which is now making our lives more confusing. The whole European Union will have to invest more in the future of young people, in development and innovation, in transport, in the environment and in a number of other areas. In many both strategic and other important areas, the European Commissioners have a key role to play, and so far 28 other Member States. It is particularly important that the Government of the Republic of Slovenia proposes a professionally competent effective and experienced candidate for the members of the European Commission to work for its successful functioning. It's all about a complex choice that we can't change so quickly. The Commission will have to make an urgent effort to reduce the democratic deficit by increasing the involvement of citizens in the policy-making of the European Union. But to reduce the democratic deficit only with the proposed amendments to the proposal, we believe that it would contribute to the longevity and complexity of the process of appointing a Slovenian candidate for Commissioner. The situation is not perfect, but the current situation does not meet democratic standards, but of course we cannot agree. Such a proposal before us would ultimately violate Article 4 of the Treaty on European Union, which speaks of mutual cooperation on the basis of the principle of loyal cooperation. There could also be a violation of Article 17, which deals with the phases of the proposal and the appointment of the President and the President of the Commission and its members – I am speaking in the event that the selection process would become too complicated with us and that the Republic of Slovenia would not have its candidate in time by September or October, when the hearings for future Commissioners in the European Parliament are already under way. It should be pointed out that the European Members, after this Article 17 of the Treaty on European Union, are not involved in the proposal of the President or President of the European Commission, but only vote on it within the European Parliament, of which they are part. Nor are they involved in adopting a list of other members of the European Commission, such as Commissioners. And according to the proposal and what has been seen so far, it is almost too optimistic to expect that most Slovenian MEPs will be able to unify quickly with regard to the candidate's proposal. Well, then the next phase would follow, where consensus would be needed by most Slovenian MEPs, which would be forwarded to the Government. So our government would no longer be independent under the Constitution. The Government's proposal for a candidate for Commissioner would have no value if it had not obtained the necessary agreement. After all, Slovenian MEPs are not part of any of the branches of government in the Republic of Slovenia. In 19 Member States of the European Union, the Government or the executive branch of the government play a key role in appointing a Commissioner. In some Member States, however, the procedure may be different under their constitutional arrangements, as the national Parliament is also involved in the second phase of the procedure. The entry of Article 17 of the Treaty on European Union clearly provides that the Council shall vote on a proposal from the members of the Commission. The Council shall have a decision in the hands of the governments of the Member States. From the whole point of view, it would not be prudent to introduce a practice that is untested and not known in any of the Member States of the European Union. Similarly in 2014, the Committee on European Union Affairs decided on similar amendments to the Commissioner's appointment procedure and disagreed with amendments to the Slovenian Commissioner's choice. The proposal for a candidate for a member of the Commission is thus always in the hands of the Government or the executive authority of the Member States. Thus, we cannot support this amendment and the addition of the proposed Law on Cooperation between the Parliament and the Government in matters of the European Union.

If we love Members in the European Parliament directly on the basis of the general and the same electoral right for a period of five years, the composition of the European Commission shall be governed by other rules, as already mentioned in Article 17 of the Treaty on European Union. The basic criterion referred to in that Article states that the members of the Commission shall be selected on the basis of their general competence and commitment to Europe between persons whose independence is unequivocal and shall not require or receive instructions from individual governments, institutions, bodies, offices or agencies. While the European Parliament, on the basis of the proposal submitted by the European Council by an absolute majority, decides on the proposed President of the European Commission, it is the President-elect who makes key decisions on the composition of his team on the basis of proposals submitted by the Member States. The European Parliament has the power to vote and decide not on individual candidates, but on the European Commission as a whole. Finally, the European Commission formally appoints the European Council. Where the basic treaties on which the European Union is founded clearly define the procedure for appointing the European Commission, there is no uniform practice in the selection of national Commissioners candidates in the Member States. Events five years ago showed that we need a formal and transparent procedure for selecting a Slovenian candidate or candidate for a member and a member of the European Commission. To this end, Article 11b was added to the Cooperation Act between the National Assembly and the Government on matters of the European Union in 2015, according to which it has key powers in the selection of the Government, but it also includes the National Assembly. The presentation of a candidate or candidate on the Committee on European Union Affairs, on the basis of which the Committee adopts an opinion and transmits it to the Government, in the opinion of the Social Democrats, duly reflects the formal relationship between the legislation on the one hand and the executive branch of the Authority on the other hand in the Republic of Slovenia. It also takes account of the legal order of the European Union. We will try this procedure for the first time in practice, and only on the basis of this experience will we be able to reflect on possible additional legal and other changes. We are social democrats, and we will always be on the side of strengthening transparency and democratic procedures, too, or especially in the case of the appointment of the highest-ranking function holders in the European Union, but it is not sufficient for us to agree on a proposal for European Commissioners or Commissioners by the majority of newly elected Members and Members, in our view, to obtain a more democratic approach, but rather to reduce it. There is also no guarantee of selecting the most competent candidate or candidate to embody European values and enjoy an appropriate international reputation. In order to comply with this criterion, there is sufficient political will, while at the same time, unlike the New Slovenia proposal, it does not interfere in any way with the system of division of power and the independence of the Government in the performance of its functions. Our choice for the best candidate according to these criteria and other formal conditions is clear and well-known to all. ParlaMint-SI_2019-06-18-SDZ8-Redna-09.ana.u132	Thank you very much, Mr Vice President. Good-bye, everybody! In the first place, recent complications with regard to the withdrawal or non-removal of Slovenian troops from missions in Lebanon and Mali have once again highlighted the lack of transparency and irresponsibility of deciding on the directions of the Slovenian military international mission and operation. This decision takes place behind closed doors, hidden from the eyes of the public, which is ultimately informed only through the media of the decisions taken. The National Assembly, as the supreme body through which the people exercise power through their representatives, elected in general, equal, direct and secret elections, has no influence on decisions on where the Slovenian armed forces, the Slovenian soldiers, will engage abroad. This is despite the fact that these referrals are one of the more visible and also more important instruments for the implementation of the foreign and security policy of the country. Following the current regulation on government referrals, the National Assembly is only informed through its working bodies, although Article 92 of the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia makes it clear that the use of defence forces is decided by the National Assembly. With the amendment to the Defence Act proposed in Levica, it is proposed to amend the decision-making process on the deployment of Slovenian Armed Forces units into international operations. The power to do so shall be delegated to the National Assembly, from which the Government shall be required to obtain agreement for each deployment of units in international operations or for the execution of the preparation and tasks of joint defence under the NATO Pact. In addition, the National Assembly is also empowered to decide on the general multiannual guidelines for the participation of the Slovenian Armed Forces and other defence and civilian capacities in international operations and missions. This strengthens the transparency and democraticity of the decision to deploy military units to military operations abroad or their integration into the multinational units and activities of the NATO Pact. Parliament's competence to take decisions on the deployment of armed forces to international operations and missions abroad is already the practice of many countries, including the members of the European Union and the NATO Pact. Among the first ones, we can point out Germany, where every deployment of soldiers abroad requires Parliament's consent. However, the German Constitutional Court has designated the army as a more parliamentary army than part of the executive branch of power. The same is true in Norway, the Czech Republic and Denmark. Although the Italian Constitution does not explicitly provide for Parliament's mandatory consent, it provides for a mandatory government consultation with Parliament prior to the decision to send an army abroad. It is similar in Sweden, where the government can only use the military to deter an attack on Swedish territory, and in other cases only with Parliament's consent, if provided for by law or an international treaty. In fact, the practice of countries where parliamentary democracy is in force is largely such that Parliament's consent is required for the decision to send a mission. In Levica, with the amendment of the Defence Act, we propose to introduce the same system to us. The fact is that it is probably because of this lack of transparency and non-democratic decision-making on the directions of the Slovenian Armed Forces abroad that Slovenia has so far engaged on the international scene mainly in missions that do not strengthen international peace and security, nor do they pursue a culture of peace and non-violence on which our security policy is to be based under Article 124 of the Constitution. The Slovenian army was sent abroad through the NATO Pact and the European Union dictatorship, where it acted as an auxiliary army of the largest powers within these two organisations and, of course, helped to realize their imperialist interests. The amendments to the law we are proposing in Levica will not necessarily change that in themselves. However, by establishing greater transparency in decision-making and introducing at least a minimum public debate on which missions the country will travel its forces, they will help to establish a clearer system of political responsibility for the decisions taken and the consequences arising therefrom.
